<Info>
**New to Corsair?** See [API access](/concepts/api), [authentication](/concepts/auth), and [error handling](/concepts/error-handling).
</Info>

## Exports

### download

`exports.download`

Download a ready export as a .tar.gz (returned as a file)

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.exports.download({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `contentBase64` | `string` | No | The file, base64-encoded. Missing when the file came back as text or contentOmitted is set. |
| `text` | `string` | No | The file as text, in place of contentBase64, when it is a text file (CSV, JSON, Markdown, XML and the like) in UTF-8. |
| `contentType` | `string` | Yes | Media type of the file, for example application/pdf. |
| `byteLength` | `number` | Yes | Size of the file in bytes. |
| `filename` | `string` | No | The file name, when known. |
| `contentOmitted` | `not_requested \| over_limit` | No | Set when the content was left out: the caller did not ask for it, or the file was over the limit. |
| `fileId` | `string` | No | Where the host saved the file. Over the limit it comes in place of the content; a host that keeps every file sets it beside the content too. On Corsair it is a Files id (fil_…): pass it to another app wherever that app takes a file URL, or read a text file or PDF with files.read_text. |



---

### get

`exports.get`

Get an export

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.exports.get({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `bytes` | `number \| null` | No | The archive's size, once ready. |
| `createdAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `error` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `expiresAt` | `string \| null` | No | When a ready archive is deleted, 7 days after it was built and never after the workspace's data. |
| `fileName` | `string \| null` | No | The archive's download name: `<name>-data-<date>.tar.gz`. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | `exp_...` |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | The sandbox's or snapshot's name when the export was asked for. |
| `orgId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `readyAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `requestedBy` | `number \| null` | No | — |
| `savedAt` | `string \| null` | No | When the disk was saved: the sandbox's last stop or pause, or the snapshot. |
| `sha256` | `string \| null` | No | The archive's SHA-256 (hex), once ready; also its download ETag. |
| `snapshotId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | `ready`: download it. `failed`: `error` says why; ask again. `expired`: deleted; ask again while the workspace is paused or disabled. |

## Feedback

### send

`feedback.send`

File an `instabox feedback` report, which opens the account's support ticket

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.feedback.send({
	type: 'bug',
	component: 'cli',
	title: 'example',
	detail: 'example',
});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `area` | `string` | No | — |
| `client_version` | `string` | No | — |
| `command` | `string` | No | — |
| `component` | `cli \| api \| sandbox \| dashboard \| skills \| docs \| other` | Yes | — |
| `detail` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `doc_ref` | `string` | No | — |
| `error` | `string` | No | — |
| `expected` | `string` | No | — |
| `os` | `string` | No | — |
| `sandbox` | `string` | No | — |
| `severity` | `blocker \| major \| minor` | No | — |
| `title` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `type` | `bug \| feature-request \| friction \| other` | Yes | — |
| `workaround` | `string` | No | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `ticket` | `object \| null` | No | — |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="ticket full type">

```ts
{
  id?: string | null,
  url?: string | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

## Gpu Queue

### cancel

`gpuQueue.cancel`

Cancel a live GPU queue entry

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.gpuQueue.cancel({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | The entry's ID (`gq_...`). |
| `idempotency_key` | `string` | No | Idempotency-Key header: a retry with the same key returns what this key already did instead of doing it again. Leave it out and the plugin sends a new random key. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `cancelling` | `boolean \| null` | No | As in `MachineQueue`. |
| `enqueuedAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `expiresAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `gpuType` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `gpus` | `number \| null` | No | — |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `kind` | `string \| null` | No | As in `MachineQueue`. |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `machineName` | `string \| null` | No | The sandbox's name; absent once it is deleted. |
| `orgId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `position` | `number \| null` | No | As in `MachineQueue`; absent unless queued. |
| `reason` | `string \| null` | No | Why an ended entry ended: `queue_expired`; `user` (a cancel or a stop) or `machine_deleted` for a cancel; for `failed`, the refusal's error code. |
| `resolvedAt` | `string \| null` | No | When the entry ended; absent while live. |
| `state` | `string \| null` | No | `queued` and `dispatched` are live; `placed` (the sandbox ran), `expired`, `cancelled` and `failed` are ends. |



---

### list

`gpuQueue.list`

List an org's GPU queue entries

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.gpuQueue.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `orgId` | `string` | No | The org whose entries to list. |
| `state` | `queued \| dispatched \| placed \| expired \| cancelled \| failed` | No | Only the entries in this state. |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Page size (1-200, default 50). |
| `cursor` | `string` | No | Opaque `nextCursor` from a previous page. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `entries` | `object[] \| null` | No | — |
| `nextCursor` | `string \| null` | No | Pass as `cursor` for the next page; absent on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="entries full type">

```ts
{
  cancelling?: boolean | null,
  enqueuedAt?: string | null,
  expiresAt?: string | null,
  gpuType?: string | null,
  gpus?: number | null,
  id?: string | null,
  kind?: string | null,
  machineId?: string | null,
  machineName?: string | null,
  orgId?: string | null,
  position?: number | null,
  reason?: string | null,
  resolvedAt?: string | null,
  state?: string | null
}[] | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

## Keys

### create

`keys.create`

Create an API key. Signed-in sessions only; an API key gets 403 permission_denied

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.keys.create({
	name: 'OPENAI_API_KEY',
	scopes: ['machines:read'],
});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `expiresInDays` | `number` | No | Days until the key expires, 1-365. 0 or absent: it does not expire. |
| `name` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `scopes` | `(machines:read \| machines:write \| machines:delete \| images:read \| images:write \| images:delete)[]` | Yes | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `apiKey` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `key` | `string \| null` | No | The secret, shown only in this reply and to polls repeated within the 10-minute delivery window. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="apiKey full type">

```ts
{
  createdAt?: string | null,
  createdBy?: number | null,
  createdByLogin?: string | null,
  expiresAt?: string | null | null,
  id?: string | null,
  lastUsedAt?: string | null | null,
  name?: string | null,
  orgId?: string | null,
  prefix?: string | null,
  scopes?: string[] | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



---

### list

`keys.list`

List your personal org's API keys (expired ones included, revoked ones not), as `GET /v1/orgs/{id}/keys` for it. Signed-in sessions only

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.keys.list({});
```

**Input:** _empty object_


**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `keys` | `object[] \| null` | No | — |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="keys full type">

```ts
{
  createdAt?: string | null,
  createdBy?: number | null,
  createdByLogin?: string | null,
  expiresAt?: string | null | null,
  id?: string | null,
  lastUsedAt?: string | null | null,
  name?: string | null,
  orgId?: string | null,
  prefix?: string | null,
  scopes?: string[] | null
}[] | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



---

### revoke

`keys.revoke`

Revoke one of your personal org's API keys. Signed-in sessions only

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.keys.revoke({ id: 'key_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Instabox accepted the call; it answered with no body. |

## Machines

### activityLeases.release

`machines.activityLeases.release`

Release a task's activity lease

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.activityLeases.release({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', leaseID: 'build-42' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `leaseID` | `string` | Yes | Caller-chosen task identifier. Reusing it renews the same lease. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Instabox accepted the call; it answered with no body. |



---

### activityLeases.renew

`machines.activityLeases.renew`

Keep a task's machine awake for a bounded period

**Risk:** `destructive`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.activityLeases.renew({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', leaseID: 'build-42' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `leaseID` | `string` | Yes | Caller-chosen task identifier. Reusing it renews the same lease. |
| `ttlSeconds` | `number` | No | 30-3600 seconds; 0 or absent uses 300. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `expiresAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |



---

### claimOomKill

`machines.claimOomKill`

The OOM kill of a background job's processes (instabox jobs wait)

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.claimOomKill({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', cgroup: 'example', bootId: 'example' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `bootId` | `string` | Yes | The sandbox boot the job ran in (/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id): only that boot's kills are returned, since session scope names repeat after a restart. |
| `cgroup` | `string` | Yes | A background job's cgroup in the sandbox: a session scope such as /user.slice/user-1000.slice/session-3.scope. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `oomKill` | `object \| null` | No | A process the sandbox's kernel OOM killer killed. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="oomKill full type">

```ts
{
  anonRssBytes?: number | null,
  at?: string | null,
  cgroup?: boolean | null,
  pid?: number | null,
  process?: string | null,
  rssBytes?: number | null,
  uid?: number | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



---

### connectionInfo

`machines.connectionInfo`

How to reach the sandbox over SSH. No side effects

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.connectionInfo({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `capabilities` | `string[] \| null` | No | `mosh`: mosh works (a session that runs `mosh-server new` gets a relay UDP port in its `MOSH CONNECT` line). |
| `host` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `hostTrust` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `moshHost` | `string \| null` | No | Optional separate Mosh gateway host; defaults to host when absent. |
| `moshPort` | `number \| null` | No | SSH port for Mosh session bootstrap; defaults to port when absent. The UDP relay port is negotiated during bootstrap. |
| `port` | `number \| null` | No | — |
| `transport` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `username` | `string \| null` | No | The sandbox ID. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="hostTrust full type">

```ts
{
  principals?: string[] | null,
  publicKey?: string | null,
  type?: string | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

### create

`machines.create`

Create a sandbox

**Risk:** `destructive`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.create({ name: 'build-42', templateId: 'agent-browser' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `idempotency_key` | `string` | No | Idempotency-Key header: a retry with the same key returns what this key already did instead of doing it again. Leave it out and the plugin sends a new random key. |
| `acceptMinimumLease` | `boolean` | No | Accepts the Mac lease of a macOS sandbox (the template's `minimumLease`, `GET /v1/templates`): a whole Mac for the org for at least 24 hours, billed whatever the sandbox does. Required to create one: without it the create is `400 minimum_lease_required` and nothing is placed. |
| `archiveSeconds` | `number` | No | Sets `archiveAt` to now plus this many seconds: the sandbox is stopped then if it is running. 0 or absent: none. |
| `autoSuspendAfterSeconds` | `number` | No | Absent: 1800 (30 minutes). The plan decides, and a value is honoured or refused, never silently replaced: a paid sandbox takes 300-1800 (5 to 30 minutes) in whole minutes, as sent, and 0 (off) where the plan allows always on (else `403 spec_not_allowed`); below 300 (1-299), above 1800, negative or not a multiple of 60 is `400 bad_request`. The free sandbox is fixed at 1800: any other value is `403 spec_not_allowed`, a plan limit (older servers: `403 permission_denied`). A refused create makes no sandbox. The reply's `autoSuspendAfterSeconds` shows the effective value. Separately, an account an admin gave manual lifecycle (`GET /v1/me` `manualLifecycle`) sends 120-86400 idle seconds, or 0 never (docs/idle-suspend.md). |
| `deleteAfterSeconds` | `number` | No | Sets `deleteAt` to now plus this many seconds (60-86400, else 400): the sandbox is then deleted, whatever its state, so it cannot outlive a client that died (`instabox run` sets it). 0 or absent: never. A server without this field ignores it. |
| `desiredState` | `running \| stopped` | No | Default: running. |
| `directSecrets` | `object[]` | No | Direct (delivery env) Secrets from this org, each as a variable and/or a home file. Real values enter the sandbox; prefer proxy Secrets for HTTPS API keys. A fork that sends none of secrets, envSecrets and directSecrets copies its source's selection. |
| `env` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Environment variables for the whole sandbox: every login shell exports them (exec, SSH, the terminal and what they start), from the first command on and again after each start and resume. Names match `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,127}$`; `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `HOME`, `HOSTNAME`, `IFS`, `LOGNAME`, `OLDPWD`, `PATH`, `PWD`, `SHELL`, `SHLVL`, `USER`, bash's readonly `BASHOPTS`, `BASH_VERSINFO`, `EUID`, `PPID`, `SHELLOPTS` and `UID`, the session's `TERM`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, the trust roots `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`, `CURL_CA_BUNDLE` and `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, and names starting `INSTABOX_` or `SSH_` are reserved (set them per exec); values have no NUL bytes; names and values at most 32 KiB together. Not on Windows templates (400). On macOS, bash and zsh login shells export them, and zsh's special parameters (`status`, `path`, `options`, `HISTSIZE`, `USERNAME`, ...) and `ZDOTDIR` are reserved too. Fixed at create: a fork takes its source's, a create from a snapshot only its own. An exec's `envs`, a Vault item and an env Secret of the same name win. Not Secrets: anything in the sandbox can read the values, and the sandbox shows only the names (`envNames`). The values are stored encrypted at rest; a site without the Secret master key refuses env (400 bad_request). |
| `envSecrets` | `string[]` | No | Explicit env Secret names from this org; omitted or [] selects none. No all mode; null is rejected. Real values enter the sandbox. Shorthand for directSecrets entries with env = the Secret's name; not sent together with directSecrets. |
| `failFast` | `boolean` | No | When no host has room for the sandbox right now, refuse the create at once with `503 no_capacity` (`retryable`; nothing is created, there is no operation and the `Idempotency-Key` is not kept) instead of answering 202 with an operation that then fails with `no_capacity`. Every other check and the rest of the create are unchanged; a host that had room can still fill up before the operation places the sandbox. The check does not ask for a host with the secret proxy, which a create with `secrets` or `envSecrets` needs: if only hosts without it have room, the create is accepted and its operation fails with `no_capacity`. A create from a snapshot that no host can start now is refused too, instead of being created stopped. A server without this field ignores it, or, if it already refuses unknown body keys, answers `400 unknown_field`. |
| `gpu` | `string` | No | GPUs for the sandbox: `TYPE` (one GPU) or `TYPE:COUNT`, such as `l4` or `l4:2`, a type of the GPU template's `gpuTypes` (`GET /v1/templates`). GPU sandboxes are for paid plans: a free org's create is `403 template_not_allowed`. The count is one of the type's `counts`: the powers of two up to its `maxPerMachine`, such as 1, 2 or 4 (a count of 3 would leave a 4-GPU host one GPU that no pair fits). An org's running sandboxes hold at most 20 GPUs in all (`403 template_limit` past it, also on a start, resume or wake; a stopped or paused sandbox holds none). The template defaults to `gpu`, and the size comes from the type: one of its `sizes`, per GPU (`l4:2` gets twice the vCPUs and memory, the same disk; `vcpus` and `memMiB` pick another of its sizes, multiplied). An unknown type, a template without GPU support or a count not in `counts` (`l4:3`) is `400 bad_request` naming the counts. With `snapshotId` of a GPU sandbox's snapshot, the snapshot's GPU type with any of its `counts` (`l4:4` from a one-GPU snapshot), sized from that count; another type is `400 bad_request`, and absent `gpu` gets the snapshot's own count (a snapshot of a 3-GPU sandbox, from before the rule, is `400 bad_request` until `gpu` names one of the counts). GPUs are billed per GPU-hour while the sandbox runs, on top of its vCPUs and memory. Absent: no GPU (from a snapshot: the snapshot's GPU). |
| `guestUser` | `string` | No | The sandbox's Linux user (`Machine.guestUser`), so `ssh <sandbox>` over your own network (Tailscale) logs in as the same name as on your computer. Lowercase letters, digits, `_` and `-`, starting with a letter or `_`, at most 32 characters; reserved names (`root`, `instabox`, the Debian base system's users and groups such as `sudo`, `systemd-*`) are refused with `400 invalid_guest_user`. Clients send the local username normalized (`DOMAIN\\alice` and `alice@corp.example` become `alice`, lowercase, spaces become `_`) and nothing when that is not a valid name. Absent: `user`; with `snapshotId`, the snapshot's user. Ignored (the sandbox gets `dev`) for templates without the `guest-user` capability. Fixed at create. |
| `imageId` | `string` | No | Deprecated: `snapshotId` under its old name (both: they must be equal). The reply carries a `Deprecation` header. |
| `maxRunMinutes` | `number` | No | Maximum continuous runtime in minutes. -1 is unlimited; 0 sleeps immediately. Independent of idle auto-pause; whichever condition is reached first pauses the sandbox. On a free machine the service sets it and only an admin session can change it: each run starts at 120 minutes and grows with use up to 480 (commands, file access, port requests, terminal or SSH traffic and CPU load count as use; activity leases and wake calls do not): https://instabox.sh/docs/machines/auto-pause#runtime-on-the-free-plan. A free machine that is not running reports 480, the most a run can last. The response reports the current limit from runStartedAt; it can change during the run. Paid machines default to -1 and their users can change this setting. Max-run suspension starts a cooldown, returning 409 machine_cooldown with Retry-After on every wake path, except on Pro and higher plans, which have none. By default it is 5 to 20 minutes (5 plus a random 0 to 15) for the first such pause in a row on the free plan. Back-to-back pauses on the free plan rest longer (60 to 75, then 120 to 135 minutes): "Longer cooldowns on the free plan" at https://instabox.sh/docs/machines/auto-pause#longer-cooldowns-on-the-free-plan. Once cooldown ends, 0 still refuses wake with 409 max_run_disabled until changed. |
| `memMiB` | `number` | No | Like vcpus: the memory (MiB) of one of your sizes, such as 8192 with vcpus 4; 0 or a negative is `400 bad_request`. |
| `metadata` | `Record<string, string>` | No | String labels. At most 32 keys of 1-64 characters `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`, values of at most 512 characters, 8 KiB as JSON. |
| `model` | `string` | No | The Mac model of a macOS template with `models`, by its `name`: the sandbox runs on that model's Macs, at one of that model's sizes. Absent: the template's `pool`, or its first open model when that pool's model is `closed`. With `snapshotId`: the model to run the snapshot on, at that model's sizes; absent, the snapshot's own `model` unless it is closed. |
| `name` | `string` | No | 1-63 lowercase letters, digits or `-`, starting with a letter or digit; unique among the org's sandboxes. Absent or empty: `box-` and 8 random hex digits (the operation's `machineId` and the sandbox carry it). |
| `networkPolicy` | `object` | No | A sandbox's outbound network policy: where it may connect to on the internet. It only narrows: a destination the platform blocks stays blocked. Inbound access (SSH, exec, published ports) is not affected. A fork starts with its source's policy. Domains are allowed through DNS: the sandbox's resolver answers only the listed names and opens the firewall for the addresses it returns, for as long as the DNS record lives, so a program must use the system resolver, and a connection to an address that no allowed name resolved to is refused unless a range lists it. It is not strict data-loss prevention: a site that shares an address with an allowed one (a CDN) is reachable too. |
| `orgId` | `string` | No | The org the sandbox counts against and is shared with (`GET /v1/orgs`); you must be a member. Absent: your personal org, or with `snapshotId` the snapshot's org (a sandbox from a snapshot lives in the snapshot's org; another is `400 bad_request`). Secret selections always resolve within this org. The org's sandbox limit and, on the free tier, its monthly included usage apply (`429 quota_exceeded`, `402 credit_exhausted`). |
| `persistentDiskGiB` | `number` | No | Storage (`/data`) size in GiB. The disks come with the size: storage gets the size's (20 GiB on api.instabox.sh), or a snapshot's storage size when that is larger. Absent or exactly that size: accepted; any other is `400 spec_not_allowed` whose message names the size's storage next to what was asked; 0 or a negative is `400 bad_request`. To get more storage, grow it later with `PATCH /v1/machines/{id}`: a running sandbox's storage grows live, and a shrink applies while it is stopped, at its next boot. The SDKs' `diskGiB` (TypeScript) and `persistent_disk_gib` (Python) are this field; `diskGiB` in a REST body is `400 unknown_field`. |
| `pool` | `string` | No | Admins only: the host pool to place the sandbox in and no other (docs/hybrid-pools.md), "default" included. With snapshotId it must be the snapshot's pool. Absent: the user's pool assignment, else the server's placement.createPools. |
| `queue` | `object` | No | Wait in the GPU queue, or for a macOS sandbox in the Mac queue (`CreateMachineRequest.queue`, `StartMachineRequest.queue`); `{}` waits up to an hour for a GPU, up to 7 days for a Mac. |
| `rootDiskGiB` | `number` | No | System files size in GiB. Like persistentDiskGiB: system files get the template's size (or the snapshot's); naming any other is `400 spec_not_allowed` (the message names the size's root disk), 0 or a negative `400 bad_request`; grow it later with `PATCH /v1/machines/{id}`. |
| `secrets` | `all \| string[]` | No | Proxy selection only. All includes current and future proxy Secrets in the sandbox's org. Explicit names resolve to stable IDs. An empty array selects none. Deleting and recreating a name does not restore an explicit grant. Null is rejected. |
| `snapshotId` | `string` | No | Create from this snapshot of yours: its system files and storage (empty storage for a system-files-only snapshot). |
| `templateId` | `string` | No | The template to create from (`GET /v1/templates`, one with `creatable` true), optionally with a version as `id@version` (e.g. `agent-browser@2026-10-15.4`, the same as `templateVersion`). Absent (and no `snapshotId` or `templateVersion`): agent-browser. An unknown or retired one is refused with `400 bad_request` whose message lists the templates your plan can create from. The free plan creates from agent-browser, android, omarchy or ci only; another template is refused with `403 template_not_allowed`. A create from a snapshot is not limited. |
| `templateVersion` | `string` | No | Default: the version `GET /v1/templates` marks `latest`. `templateId` may name it instead, as `id@version` (e.g. `agent-browser@2026-10-15.4`); both at once must agree. A version the template does not have is `400 bad_request` whose message lists its versions, newest first, the `latest` (or `default`) one tagged. |
| `vault` | `boolean` | No | False disables legacy Vault delivery. Org Secret selections are independent. |
| `vcpus` | `number` | No | Absent: the default size (4 vCPUs, 8 GiB memory; `limits.machineSpec`). From a snapshot with `sizeLocked` (not of a GPU sandbox): the vCPUs and memory of the sandbox it was saved from (the snapshot's `defaultSpec`), absent or named; any other is `400 spec_not_allowed` stating the snapshot's size, and that size is `403 spec_not_allowed` while the org's plan does not offer it (upgrade the plan). A snapshot without `sizeLocked` (saved before this rule) takes any size below. Else one of the org's sizes (`GET /v1/orgs` `allowedSpecs`: the default, its plan's others and any size an admin granted the org; on api.instabox.sh free 4/8192 only, Pro 2/4096, 4/8192, 8/16384, Builder 2/4096 up to 16/32768, Team and Enterprise 2/4096 up to 32/65536), or, from a template with a `pool` (macOS, Windows), its `defaultSpec` or one of its `sizes`, whatever the plan; name memMiB too when two of them have this many vCPUs. Anything else is refused with `400 spec_not_allowed`; 0 or a negative (no size) with `400 bad_request`. |

Absent: a host from before that (during a rolling control deploy), which gave the fork its defaults with only the request's `metadata` and `deleteAfterSeconds`; set the idle auto-suspend and max run with `PATCH /v1/machines/{id}`. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `machine` | `object \| null` | No | The sandbox as it is when `GET /v1/operations/{id}` is read. Included when the operation `succeeded`, the sandbox still exists, and the caller may read sandboxes (`machines:read` for API keys). Later operations can change it; it is not a record of the state at completion. Absent otherwise, and from older servers. |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `phase` | `string \| null` | No | Free text progress, e.g. scheduling, booting, waiting_ready, snapshotting. |
| `scheduledAt` | `string \| null` | No | The stop or delete of a macOS sandbox inside its Mac lease's minimum is not done now but at this time (the sandbox's `archiveAt` or `deleteAt`); the operation is `succeeded`. |
| `state` | `string \| null` | No | `succeeded` and `failed` are terminal. |
| `trigger` | `string \| null` | No | Set when instabox started the operation itself: `idle` (paused when idle), `max_run` (maximum continuous runtime), `memory_pressure` (paused, its processes kept, because the host it ran on was running out of memory; the next use wakes it, as after `idle`), `request` (wake on use), `wake` (POST /v1/machines/&#123;id&#125;/wake), `timeout`, `archive`, `delete_at` (the sandbox's `deleteAt` passed), `drain` (maintenance by an admin), `user_delete` (an admin deleted the owner), `user_disable` (an admin disabled the owner), `admin` (an admin stopped or paused the sandbox), `admin_delete` (an admin deleted the sandbox), `credit` (its free-tier org used up the month's included usage), `spend_cap`, `downgrade`, `subscription_ended`, `payment_method` (its free workspace has no card on file), `members` (its free workspace has more members than the free plan allows), `early_allowance_ended` (paused because the account's early free-sandbox allowance ended and it has more free sandboxes than the free plan runs), `abuse_pool` (paused because the sandbox tried to connect to a blocked destination on the egress blocklist, such as a crypto-mining pool; a DNS lookup of a listed domain never suspends) or `abuse_cpu` (paused by the CPU abuse detector: a restricted account's sandbox ran flat out for an hour with no memory use, no public port, nobody using it and a connection to a mining port) or `abuse_scan` (paused by the flow detector: a restricted account's sandbox swept one port across many hosts, scanned many ports of one host or brute-forced a login port). Absent: a client request. |
| `twin` | `object \| null` | No | On the answer to a half-size macOS create that opened a Mac: the second sandbox the create made on it (a Mac runs two sandboxes of half its size). |
| `type` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `updatedAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |

### createAccess

`machines.createAccess`

Get a Terminal or VNC connection URL and short-lived admission ticket

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.createAccess({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', kind: 'terminal' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `kind` | `terminal \| vnc` | Yes | — |
| `requireTicket` | `boolean` | No | Issue a ticket even when routing points to the combined API, for reconnecting an existing access watch. |
| `webUrl` | `string` | No | Optional access origin for pre-cutover validation. Requires an administrator sign-in (API keys cannot use it). Only this response targets that origin; global routing is unchanged. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `expiresAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `protocol` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `token` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `url` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `watchUrl` | `string \| null` | No | — |

### createSnapshot

`machines.createSnapshot`

Save a sandbox's system files and storage as a snapshot

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.createSnapshot({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', name: 'OPENAI_API_KEY' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `idempotency_key` | `string` | No | Idempotency-Key header: a retry with the same key returns what this key already did instead of doing it again. Leave it out and the plugin sends a new random key. |
| `description` | `string` | No | — |
| `forFork` | `boolean` | No | Saved to fork its source (`instabox fork`, when the native fork cannot run). For a free source, the server records its max-run window as activity extended it, and a sandbox the same user creates from this snapshot in the next 30 minutes starts with that window, as a native fork does. Names no window. |
| `name` | `string` | Yes | Unique among the workspace's snapshots; 1-128 letters, digits, '.', '_' or '-', starting with a letter or digit and not with sn_ or im_. |
| `rootOnly` | `boolean` | No | Save the system files only, not the storage (`/data`). |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `createdAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `dataSizeBytes` | `number \| null` | No | The logical size of the storage disk (`/data`) a sandbox created from it gets, not the bytes stored (the saved data is sparse and compressed); absent for system-files-only snapshots. Sandboxes created from it get at least this much storage. |
| `defaultSpec` | `object \| null` | No | The size a sandbox created from this snapshot gets when the create names none: with `sizeLocked`, the vCPUs and memory of the sandbox it was saved from, the only size a create from it may name; without (saved before this rule), the default size (`limits.machineSpec`, 4 vCPU / 8 GiB on api.instabox.sh); a GPU snapshot: its GPU type's size for the count, which a create may change with `gpu`. Plus the storage (`/data`) and system disk sizes the snapshot was saved with, which a created sandbox gets at least. Absent when its template is unknown. |
| `description` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `error` | `string \| null` | No | Why a `failed` snapshot failed. |
| `exclusiveBytes` | `number \| null` | No | No longer computed; servers leave it out. (Was the stored bytes only this snapshot references.) |
| `guestUser` | `string \| null` | No | The source sandbox's Linux user (`Machine.guestUser`), which a sandbox created from this snapshot keeps unless the create names another. `dev` for snapshots of sandboxes from older templates. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | `sn_...` (`im_...` for older snapshots). |
| `model` | `string \| null` | No | The Mac model (`TemplateModel.name`) a macOS snapshot was saved on. A create from it runs there unless it names another `model` or that model is closed. |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Unique among the org's live snapshots. |
| `orgId` | `string \| null` | No | The org the snapshot belongs to (its source sandbox's). |
| `publishedAt` | `string \| null` | No | When the visibility became community or official. |
| `publisherLogin` | `string \| null` | No | Who shared or published it. |
| `rootSizeBytes` | `number \| null` | No | The logical size of the system disk a sandbox created from it gets (its provisioned capacity), not the bytes stored: a snapshot is saved sparse and compressed. |
| `sizeLocked` | `boolean \| null` | No | True for a snapshot (not of a GPU sandbox) saved since a create from a snapshot gets its source's size one to one: a create from it gets `defaultSpec`'s vCPUs and memory only (another is `400 spec_not_allowed`). Absent for older snapshots, which create at any size. |
| `sourceMachineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `sourceSnapshotSeq` | `number \| null` | No | The source sandbox's saved state (`storage.snapshotSeq`) this snapshot was taken from; 0 for a live snapshot of a sandbox that was never stopped. |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | `ready` can be used. A snapshot of a running sandbox is `pending` while it uploads (its sizes are the sandbox's until then), then `ready` or `failed`. |
| `template` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `visibility` | `string \| null` | No | Who can create from the snapshot (docs/community-templates.md): `private` (the default, its org's members), `community` (every signed-in account) or `official` (listed next to the built-in templates). Absent on older servers: treat as private. Handlers do not set community or official yet. |

### delete

`machines.delete`

Delete a sandbox, its system files and its storage

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.delete({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

| `confirm_delete` | `string` | No | X-Confirm-Delete header: the ID or exact name of what is deleted. Left out, the plugin sends the id from the path. |
| `now` | `boolean` | No | `true` deletes a macOS sandbox at once inside its Mac lease's minimum, which is still billed. Without it such a delete is scheduled for the minimum's end (the sandbox's `deleteAt`, the operation's `scheduledAt`). No effect on other sandboxes. |

Later operations can change it; it is not a record of the state at completion. Absent otherwise, and from older servers. |

| `state` | `string \| null` | No | `succeeded` and `failed` are terminal. |

Absent: a client request. |

### desktop.get

`machines.desktop.get`

The state of a running machine's desktop; install or start it

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.desktop.get({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `action` | `status \| install \| start \| takeover \| handback` | No | — |
| `height` | `number` | No | Screen height in pixels for start, 600-1600 (default 800). |
| `width` | `number` | No | Screen width in pixels for start, 800-2560 (default 1280). |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `actions` | `object[] \| null` | No | The computer-use MCP's last tool calls (at most 20, oldest first), as its proxy logged them. |
| `agentPausable` | `boolean \| null` | No | The sandbox's computer-use MCP honors the control (its wrapper runs the server through the proxy). False on a sandbox made before the proxy existed: a takeover would not pause its agent, so clients should offer watching only. |
| `controlError` | `string \| null` | No | The control file in the sandbox could not be written (its runtime directory is missing or not writable): takeover and handback cannot take effect. The reason, from the sandbox. |
| `controller` | `string \| null` | No | Who drives the desktop: `agent` (the default; viewers watch) or `human` (a viewer took over, and the computer-use MCP refuses input until they hand back or 30 s pass without a call from them). |
| `failed` | `boolean \| null` | No | The last install failed; log holds its last lines. |
| `hint` | `string \| null` | No | Only on a `start` that started nothing because the desktop is not installed (`start` never installs), is still installing, or failed to install: what to send next. Absent otherwise. |
| `installed` | `boolean \| null` | No | — |
| `installing` | `boolean \| null` | No | — |
| `kind` | `string \| null` | No | The desktop the sandbox has. `vm` (a Windows or Mac sandbox): its VM's own screen, always running and controlled by every viewer (`controller` `human`, `yours`); `install`, `start`, `takeover` and `handback` change nothing. `x11` (absent from servers before this field): Xvnc and Xfce, installed on first use, driven by the computer-use MCP. `wayland` (the omarchy template): a compositor that starts at boot and is in the image: `install` changes nothing, `start` only brings the session and its VNC server up and sizes the display, and there is no computer-use MCP (`agentPausable` is false); watch and use it through VNC. |
| `log` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `os` | `string \| null` | No | Only on `kind` `vm`: the guest OS. Clients map keys by it: on `macos`, a Mac's Command is the guest's Command (keysym Alt_L) and Option its Option (Meta_L). |
| `running` | `boolean \| null` | No | The VNC server answers: GET /v1/machines/&#123;id&#125;/vnc connects. |
| `yours` | `boolean \| null` | No | The caller (its sign-in session or API key) is the viewer in control. Absent when false. |

  } | null,

  tool?: string | null

### exec

`machines.exec`

Run a command in a running machine (cmd through bash -lc, or argv with no shell) and get exitCode, stdout and stderr

**Risk:** `destructive`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.exec({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', cmd: 'uname -a && nproc', timeoutSeconds: 60 });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters) or the sandbox's name (unique among your sandboxes; names never contain `_`, so the two never collide). Older servers take only the ID and answer `404` for a name. |
| `argv` | `string[]` | No | The program and its arguments, run with no shell: each element reaches the program exactly as sent (no expansion of `$`, `*` or `~`, no word splitting, quotes or redirections; `""` is an empty argument). `argv[0]` must not be empty and is looked up on the login profile's `PATH`; the login profile, `envs` and `cwd` apply as for `cmd`. A program that is not found exits 127, one that cannot run 126. At most 64 KiB counting each element's length plus one, no NUL bytes. The command sent to the sandbox (`envs`, `cwd` and `argv`, quoted for the shell: about 9 bytes per element plus its text) must also stay under 128 KiB, so many short elements can be `400 bad_request` below the 64 KiB cap; send bulk data on `stdin`. Set this or `cmd`. |
| `cmd` | `string` | No | Shell command, run with `bash -lc`. At most 64 KiB, no NUL bytes. Set this or `argv`; an empty `cmd` is no `cmd`. |
| `cwd` | `string` | No | Working directory (default HOME, `/data/home`). |
| `envs` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Environment variables exported before the command (after the login profile). Names match `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`. |
| `historyCommand` | `string` | No | How the command appears in the sandbox's history instead of `cmd` or `argv`, for a wrapper that runs something else (the CLI's `exec --background`). Cut at 4096 bytes like them; no NUL bytes. |
| `noHistory` | `boolean` | No | Leave the command out of the sandbox's history (`GET /v1/machines/{id}/history`): for a client's own housekeeping commands, such as the CLI's job polls. Does not go with `historyCommand`. |
| `secretEnvs` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Direct (delivery env) Secrets of the sandbox's org exported for this command only: variable name -&gt; Secret name. Resolved at exec time, never stored or written into the sandbox; each use is audited by name. A name may not also be in `envs`; reserved variables are refused. A proxy or missing Secret is 400. Only where `GET /v1/me` has `directSecretRefs`; elsewhere 400 (a control host from before secretEnvs would run the command without them). |
| `stdin` | `string` | No | Text fed to the command, then EOF. It is a JSON string, so the command reads its UTF-8 bytes: NUL, CR and LF arrive unchanged (escaped as `\\u0000`, `\\r`, `\\n`). At most 1 MiB (1048576 bytes) of UTF-8, counted in bytes, not characters (`é` is 2): more is `400 bad_request` `stdin is longer than 1048576 bytes`. The schema sets no `maxLength` here, because `maxLength` counts characters. JSON cannot carry bytes that are not UTF-8; any in the request body reach the command as U+FFFD (`ef bf bd`), with no error. For binary input, send it base64-encoded and decode it in the command (`base64 -d \| ...`; up to 768 KiB of binary fits), or write the file first with `PUT /v1/machines/{id}/files` and redirect it in (`cmd < file`). The CLI's `exec --stdin` does that upload itself for input past 1 MiB or not UTF-8, up to 5 GiB. |
| `timeoutSeconds` | `number` | No | Default 60, max 600. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `diskWarning` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `exitCode` | `number \| null` | No | Exit status; 128+N if killed by standard signal N, otherwise 255 with `signal` set; 124 if timed out. |
| `invalidUtf8` | `boolean \| null` | No | stdout or stderr had bytes that are not UTF-8 (binary output), replaced with U+FFFD, so the output is not what the command wrote. Absent when false; also set on a streamed exec's exit event. |
| `oomKill` | `object \| null` | No | A process the sandbox's kernel OOM killer killed. |
| `oomKilled` | `boolean \| null` | No | The command exited non-zero and the sandbox's kernel OOM killer killed one of its own processes (the command or a child: its SSH session's cgroup; never another command's process, and each kill is reported once). `oomKill` says which, when the sandbox named it. Checked only for exit code 137, a signal, or stderr saying a process was killed. False when none was seen, the exec's session scope could not be read, or the sandbox's host or guest agent cannot report kills; a kill whose kernel log record was lost is still true when the session scope's oom_kill count shows it (`oomKill` absent). Absent from older servers. Also set on a streamed exec's exit event. |
| `signal` | `string \| null` | No | The signal that killed the command, as the sandbox named it (absent otherwise). |
| `stderr` | `string \| null` | No | First 1 MiB of standard error. |
| `stdout` | `string \| null` | No | First 1 MiB of standard output. |
| `timedOut` | `boolean \| null` | No | The command ran past `timeoutSeconds` and was killed. |
| `truncated` | `boolean \| null` | No | stdout or stderr was longer than 1 MiB and was cut. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="diskWarning full type">

```ts
{
  level?: string | null,
  message?: string | null,
  mount?: string | null,
  noSpace?: boolean | null,
  usedPct?: number | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="oomKill full type">

```ts
{
  anonRssBytes?: number | null,
  at?: string | null,
  cgroup?: boolean | null,
  pid?: number | null,
  process?: string | null,
  rssBytes?: number | null,
  uid?: number | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

### files.download

`machines.files.download`

Download a file's raw bytes from a running machine (returned as a file)

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.files.download({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', path: '/data/home/notes.md' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `path` | `string` | Yes | Absolute path of the file, at most 4096 bytes. |
| `range` | `string` | No | Range header, such as bytes=0-1048575, to download part of the file. |
| `if_range` | `string` | No | If-Range header: the ETag of the earlier part, so a changed file comes back whole. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `contentBase64` | `string` | No | The file, base64-encoded. Missing when the file came back as text or contentOmitted is set. |
| `text` | `string` | No | The file as text, in place of contentBase64, when it is a text file (CSV, JSON, Markdown, XML and the like) in UTF-8. |
| `contentType` | `string` | Yes | Media type of the file, for example application/pdf. |
| `byteLength` | `number` | Yes | Size of the file in bytes. |
| `filename` | `string` | No | The file name, when known. |
| `contentOmitted` | `not_requested \| over_limit` | No | Set when the content was left out: the caller did not ask for it, or the file was over the limit. |
| `fileId` | `string` | No | Where the host saved the file. Over the limit it comes in place of the content; a host that keeps every file sets it beside the content too. On Corsair it is a Files id (fil_…): pass it to another app wherever that app takes a file URL, or read a text file or PDF with files.read_text. |

### fork

`machines.fork`

Create a copy of a sandbox on its host, without a snapshot

**Risk:** `destructive`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.fork({
	id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd',
	networkPolicy: { outbound: 'restricted' },
});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `idempotency_key` | `string` | No | Idempotency-Key header: a retry with the same key returns what this key already did instead of doing it again. Leave it out and the plugin sends a new random key. |
| `acceptMinimumLease` | `boolean` | No | As in CreateMachineRequest: required to fork a macOS sandbox (`400 minimum_lease_required` without it). |
| `autoSuspendAfterSeconds` | `number` | No | As in CreateMachineRequest. Absent: the source's (0, off, when it has none), checked against the plan as a create's. |
| `deleteAfterSeconds` | `number` | No | As in CreateMachineRequest (60-86400). Absent or 0: the source's delete deadline, the same instant (none if the source has none); a source due within 60 seconds is then refused with 400. |
| `directSecrets` | `object[]` | No | As in CreateMachineRequest; alone, it selects no proxy Secrets. |
| `envSecrets` | `string[]` | No | As in CreateMachineRequest. |
| `maxRunMinutes` | `number` | No | As in CreateMachineRequest. Absent: the source's (the plan default when it has none), checked as a create's. |
| `memMiB` | `number` | No | — |
| `metadata` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Labels added to the source's (a key here replaces the source's), at most 31 keys here and 32 in all (`instabox-fork-of` is set to the source's ID). Absent: the source's labels. |
| `name` | `string` | No | The new sandbox's name (as in CreateMachineRequest). Absent: `<source>-fork-` and 4 random hex digits. |
| `networkPolicy` | `object` | No | A sandbox's outbound network policy: where it may connect to on the internet. It only narrows: a destination the platform blocks stays blocked. Inbound access (SSH, exec, published ports) is not affected. A fork starts with its source's policy. Domains are allowed through DNS: the sandbox's resolver answers only the listed names and opens the firewall for the addresses it returns, for as long as the DNS record lives, so a program must use the system resolver, and a connection to an address that no allowed name resolved to is refused unless a range lists it. It is not strict data-loss prevention: a site that shares an address with an allowed one (a CDN) is reachable too. |
| `secrets` | `all \| string[]` | No | Proxy selection only. All includes current and future proxy Secrets in the sandbox's org. Explicit names resolve to stable IDs. An empty array selects none. Deleting and recreating a name does not restore an explicit grant. Null is rejected. |
| `vcpus` | `number` | No | With `memMiB`, one of the org's sizes, or of the template's `defaultSpec` and `sizes` when it has a `pool`. Absent: the source's size. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="directSecrets full type">

```ts
{
  env?: string,
  file?: string,
  secret: string
}[]
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="networkPolicy full type">

```ts
{
  allowCidrs?: string[],
  allowDomains?: string[],
  outbound: restricted | open
}
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `createdAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `error` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `forkInherited` | `boolean \| null` | No | On the answer to `POST /v1/machines/{id}/fork`: the control host that took it keeps the source's delete deadline, idle auto-suspend, max run and metadata, and takes `autoSuspendAfterSeconds` and `maxRunMinutes`. Absent: a host from before that (during a rolling control deploy), which gave the fork its defaults with only the request's `metadata` and `deleteAfterSeconds`; set the idle auto-suspend and max run with `PATCH /v1/machines/{id}`. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `machine` | `object \| null` | No | The sandbox as it is when `GET /v1/operations/{id}` is read. Included when the operation `succeeded`, the sandbox still exists, and the caller may read sandboxes (`machines:read` for API keys). Later operations can change it; it is not a record of the state at completion. Absent otherwise, and from older servers. |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `phase` | `string \| null` | No | Free text progress, e.g. scheduling, booting, waiting_ready, snapshotting. |
| `scheduledAt` | `string \| null` | No | The stop or delete of a macOS sandbox inside its Mac lease's minimum is not done now but at this time (the sandbox's `archiveAt` or `deleteAt`); the operation is `succeeded`. |
| `state` | `string \| null` | No | `succeeded` and `failed` are terminal. |
| `trigger` | `string \| null` | No | Set when instabox started the operation itself: `idle` (paused when idle), `max_run` (maximum continuous runtime), `memory_pressure` (paused, its processes kept, because the host it ran on was running out of memory; the next use wakes it, as after `idle`), `request` (wake on use), `wake` (POST /v1/machines/&#123;id&#125;/wake), `timeout`, `archive`, `delete_at` (the sandbox's `deleteAt` passed), `drain` (maintenance by an admin), `user_delete` (an admin deleted the owner), `user_disable` (an admin disabled the owner), `admin` (an admin stopped or paused the sandbox), `admin_delete` (an admin deleted the sandbox), `credit` (its free-tier org used up the month's included usage), `spend_cap`, `downgrade`, `subscription_ended`, `payment_method` (its free workspace has no card on file), `members` (its free workspace has more members than the free plan allows), `early_allowance_ended` (paused because the account's early free-sandbox allowance ended and it has more free sandboxes than the free plan runs), `abuse_pool` (paused because the sandbox tried to connect to a blocked destination on the egress blocklist, such as a crypto-mining pool; a DNS lookup of a listed domain never suspends) or `abuse_cpu` (paused by the CPU abuse detector: a restricted account's sandbox ran flat out for an hour with no memory use, no public port, nobody using it and a connection to a mining port) or `abuse_scan` (paused by the flow detector: a restricted account's sandbox swept one port across many hosts, scanned many ports of one host or brute-forced a login port). Absent: a client request. |
| `twin` | `object \| null` | No | On the answer to a half-size macOS create that opened a Mac: the second sandbox the create made on it (a Mac runs two sandboxes of half its size). |
| `type` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `updatedAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |

### get

`machines.get`

Get a sandbox

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.get({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters) or the sandbox's name (unique among your sandboxes; names never contain `_`, so the two never collide). Older servers take only the ID and answer `404` for a name. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `abuseLock` | `object \| null` | No | The owner's account of the abuse check's pause behind a sandbox's cooldownReason: when, what the sandbox did in a sentence (explanation) and in detail (what, with the exact address of a blocked destination, the address and port of a brute force, the swept port or scanned host and the count of a scan, the CPU share and, when the check confirmed it, the mining program and its pool), and how to appeal. |
| `archiveAt` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the sandbox is stopped if it is running (set at creation with `archiveSeconds`): its files are kept and a start brings it back. Resuming does not clear it; a sandbox that is not running when it passes only has it cleared. Absent: none. |
| `autoSuspendAfterSeconds` | `number \| null \| null` | No | The running sandbox is paused once it has been idle this many seconds (no agent working, no terminal input or output, little CPU); null: off. Pause, resume, stop and start keep it. |
| `autoSuspendedAt` | `string \| null` | No | When the sandbox was queued to pause automatically; a successful automatic resume or its next explicit operation clears it. Absent: none. |
| `cooldownReason` | `string \| null` | No | While cooldownUntil has not passed, the automatic abuse check whose pause locks the sandbox, the same as a wake's 409 `machine_cooldown` Error.reason: `abuse_egress_blocklist` (it tried to connect to a blocked destination on the egress blocklist, not a DNS lookup), `abuse_network_flows` (scanning or attack traffic), `abuse_cpu` (mining). Absent for a max-run cooldown, and once the lock ends. |
| `cooldownUntil` | `string \| null \| null` | No | All wake paths are refused until this deadline, including admin actions: after a max-run suspension, or the 24-hour lock of an automatic abuse check's pause (cooldownReason). |
| `createdAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `createdByApp` | `object \| null` | No | The public part of an OAuth app, shown on consent pages, grants and sandboxes. |
| `deleteAt` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the sandbox is deleted, whatever its state (set at creation with `deleteAfterSeconds`). The delete is an ordinary `delete` operation (`trigger: delete_at`), queued at `deleteAt` (within about a second) once no other operation is in progress. Absent: never. |
| `desiredState` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `earlyAllowanceEnded` | `boolean \| null` | No | Set on a stopped or paused free sandbox that cannot be started because its org's early free-sandbox allowance ended and as many free sandboxes as the standard limit allows are running: a start is refused (`402 early_allowance_ended`) until one is paused or deleted. Absent otherwise. |
| `envNames` | `string[] \| null` | No | The names of the sandbox's create-time `env`, sorted. Absent when it has none. The values are never shown: read them in the sandbox. |
| `free` | `boolean \| null` | No | A free-tier org's sandbox (docs/pricing.md): never billed, pauses after 30 idle minutes (another `autoSuspendAfterSeconds` is refused with 403). On a paid plan every sandbox is billed. |
| `guestUser` | `string \| null` | No | The Linux user in the sandbox that SSH sessions, exec and the terminal log in as, fixed at create: the `guestUser` the create named, else `user` (a create from a snapshot: the snapshot's user). `dev` on sandboxes from templates without the `guest-user` capability, and on every sandbox created before the field. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `image` | `object \| null` | No | Deprecated: `snapshot`, under its old name (same value). |
| `lastError` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `lastOom` | `object \| null` | No | A process the sandbox's kernel OOM killer killed. |
| `lease` | `object \| null` | No | The Mac lease a macOS sandbox runs under. Absent on every other sandbox, and while a macOS sandbox runs under none (stopped after its lease ended). |
| `maxRunMinutes` | `number \| null` | No | Maximum continuous runtime in minutes. -1 is unlimited; 0 sleeps immediately. Independent of idle auto-pause; whichever condition is reached first pauses the sandbox. On a free machine the service sets it and only an admin session can change it: each run starts at 120 minutes and grows with use up to 480 (commands, file access, port requests, terminal or SSH traffic and CPU load count as use; activity leases and wake calls do not): https://instabox.sh/docs/machines/auto-pause#runtime-on-the-free-plan. A free machine that is not running reports 480, the most a run can last. The response reports the current limit from runStartedAt; it can change during the run. Paid machines default to -1 and their users can change this setting. Max-run suspension starts a cooldown, returning 409 machine_cooldown with Retry-After on every wake path, except on Pro and higher plans, which have none. By default it is 5 to 20 minutes (5 plus a random 0 to 15) for the first such pause in a row on the free plan. Back-to-back pauses on the free plan rest longer (60 to 75, then 120 to 135 minutes): "Longer cooldowns on the free plan" at https://instabox.sh/docs/machines/auto-pause#longer-cooldowns-on-the-free-plan. Once cooldown ends, 0 still refuses wake with 409 max_run_disabled until changed. |
| `metadata` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | String labels. At most 32 keys of 1-64 characters `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`, values of at most 512 characters, 8 KiB as JSON. |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `networkPolicy` | `object \| null` | No | A sandbox's outbound network policy: where it may connect to on the internet. It only narrows: a destination the platform blocks stays blocked. Inbound access (SSH, exec, published ports) is not affected. A fork starts with its source's policy. Domains are allowed through DNS: the sandbox's resolver answers only the listed names and opens the firewall for the addresses it returns, for as long as the DNS record lives, so a program must use the system resolver, and a connection to an address that no allowed name resolved to is refused unless a range lists it. It is not strict data-loss prevention: a site that shares an address with an allowed one (a CDN) is reachable too. |
| `oomKills` | `number \| null` | No | Processes the sandbox's kernel OOM killer killed since the sandbox was created (out of memory, or a memory cgroup's limit). Read from the sandbox about once a minute while it runs, and after an exec that exits non-zero; 0 on sandboxes whose host or guest agent predates the reading (absent from older servers). |
| `operationId` | `string \| null \| null` | No | The active operation, if any. |
| `orgId` | `string \| null` | No | The org the sandbox counts against (docs/orgs.md). |
| `queue` | `object \| null` | No | A sandbox's live GPU queue or Mac queue entry (`Machine.queue`). A GPU entry has `gpuType` and `gpus`; a Mac entry has `pool`. |
| `readyAt` | `string \| null` | No | When the running sandbox last became ready (`runtime.ready` turned true: SSH, exec and files work). For a new sandbox, `readyAt` minus `createdAt` is its create time; after a start or resume it is that start's. Absent while the sandbox is not ready, and on a sandbox that was already ready before the field existed (until its next start or resume). |
| `revision` | `number \| null` | No | Increases with each accepted change. |
| `runStartedAt` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Start of the current continuous run, conservatively initialized on a first error observation too; resets only after confirmed sleep. |
| `runtime` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `snapshot` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `spec` | `object \| null` | No | A sandbox's size. |
| `state` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `storage` | `object \| null` | No | The sandbox's storage (`/data`, where HOME lives; `/` on macOS, whose disk is the root volume). |
| `suspendable` | `boolean \| null` | No | false: the sandbox cannot be paused, since its hosts keep no memory of a running sandbox (macOS). Stop the sandbox instead; its files are kept. Absent: it can. |
| `template` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `updatedAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `vault` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether legacy Vault delivery is enabled. Independent of org Secret selections and delivery status at the sandbox's /secrets endpoint. |

### history.get

`machines.history.get`

One past command with its stored output

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.history.get({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', execId: 'ex_3n8w1c' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters) or the sandbox's name (unique among your sandboxes; names never contain `_`, so the two never collide). Older servers take only the ID and answer `404` for a name. |
| `execId` | `string` | Yes | A command ID (`ex_…`) from the history, or `latest` for the newest command. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `command` | `string \| null` | No | The request's `cmd`, its `argv` quoted for a shell, or its `historyCommand`; cut at 4096 bytes. |
| `cwd` | `string \| null` | No | The request's `cwd` (absent: HOME). |
| `durationMs` | `number \| null` | No | — |
| `endedAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `exitCode` | `number \| null` | No | The `ExecResult`'s exit code; -1 for `lost`, `cancelled` and `unreachable`. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Command ID (`ex_…`). |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `outputTruncated` | `boolean \| null` | No | The stored output is cut: the history keeps the first 64 KiB of each stream. |
| `signal` | `string \| null` | No | The signal that killed the command, as the sandbox named it (absent otherwise). |
| `startedAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | How the command ended: `exited` (exit code reported; `signal` set if a signal killed it), `timed_out` (killed after `timeoutSeconds`), `lost` (the connection to the sandbox ended before the exit status; it may have run), `cancelled` (the client closed the request; the command was killed) or `unreachable` (the sandbox could not be reached; nothing ran). |
| `stderr` | `string \| null` | No | The stored stderr (`GET /v1/machines/{id}/history/{execId}` only). |
| `stderrBytes` | `number \| null` | No | Size of the stored stderr. |
| `stdout` | `string \| null` | No | The stored stdout (`GET /v1/machines/{id}/history/{execId}` only). |
| `stdoutBytes` | `number \| null` | No | Size of the stored stdout. |
| `streamed` | `boolean \| null` | No | The client streamed the output (`Accept: text/event-stream`). |
| `timedOut` | `boolean \| null` | No | — |
| `truncated` | `boolean \| null` | No | The exec's result had its output cut at 1 MiB per stream (never for a streamed exec). |
| `userId` | `number \| null` | No | The account that ran it. |

| `nextCursor` | `string \| null` | No | The `cursor` of the next (older) page; absent on the last page. |

### list

`machines.list`

List the sandboxes of your orgs

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.list({ limit: 20 });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `cursor` | `string` | No | Opaque `nextCursor` from a previous page. |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Page size. |
| `metadata` | `string[]` | No | `key:value` (split at the first `:`); only sandboxes whose metadata has the key with exactly that value. Repeatable; all terms must match (AND). |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `machines` | `object[] \| null` | No | — |
| `nextCursor` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Pass as `cursor` for the next page; null on the last page. |

### logs

`machines.logs`

The sandbox's console log and recent operations, to see why it did not boot or is unreachable

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.logs({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `tail` | `number` | No | Lines of console output to return (default 200, at most 5000), fewer when the lines are long (see `consoleTruncated`). Ignored with `after`. |
| `after` | `number` | No | A `cursor` from a previous response: return only the console output written since (up to 1 MiB). |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `console` | `string \| null` | No | Console output: the last `tail` lines, or with `after`, the output written since that cursor. Empty when there is none or with `consoleUnavailable`. |
| `consoleReset` | `boolean \| null` | No | `after` was past the end of the log because it was rotated and the kept previous 8 MiB no longer reach back to the cursor, so `console` starts again at the beginning of the current log and output in between is missing. A rotation the kept copy covers carries on without it. Absent when false. |
| `consoleTruncated` | `boolean \| null` | No | There is older console output than `console`: the log goes back further than `tail` lines, or than the bytes read for them (256 per line, at least 64 KiB, at most 1 MiB), so long lines give fewer than `tail`. Absent when false and with `after`. |
| `consoleUnavailable` | `string \| null` | No | Why there is no console output, e.g. the sandbox's disks are in storage or its host is not answering. Absent when the console was read. |
| `cursor` | `number \| null` | No | Where `console` ends; pass it as `after` to read only newer output. |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `operations` | `object[] \| null` | No | The last 20 operations, oldest first. |
| `state` | `string \| null` | No | The sandbox's state, as on Machine. |

### ports.create

`machines.ports.create`

Give one HTTP port of a sandbox an HTTPS URL, public or private

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.ports.create({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd', port: 3000 });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters). |
| `access` | `public \| private` | No | Omitted: the current access, or public for a new port. Changing it ends the old publication's connections. `private` is for `http` ports only. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Omitted: the current name, or "Port N" for a new port. |
| `port` | `number` | Yes | Desktop port 5901 and Orca port 6768 (plus the configured Orca port) are reserved. |
| `protocol` | `http \| tcp` | No | Omitted: the current protocol, or `http` for a new port. `tcp` publishes a raw TCP service behind TLS when the server enables it (403 otherwise; admins only while in preview); paid plans only, at most 5 per org by default (429). A port already published with the other protocol answers 409 until it is unpublished. A `tcp` port cannot be private (400). |
| `rotateToken` | `boolean` | No | With a private port: a new token, returned in the response; connections that used the old one close. 400 for a public port. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `access` | `string \| null` | No | `public`: anyone with the URL. `private`: only requests that carry the port's token (`_token` query parameter once, the cookie it sets, or `Authorization: Bearer`). |
| `address` | `string \| null` | No | `tcp` only: the TLS endpoint `host:port`; the client must send the host as SNI. psql: `host=<host> port=<port> sslmode=require`. |
| `createdAt` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `expiresAt` | `null \| null` | No | Always null (public ports do not expire); kept for older in-guest instabox-port agents. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | The publication's ID; a new one after an unpublish, an access change or a token rotation. The URL does not change. |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `port` | `number \| null` | No | — |
| `protocol` | `string \| null` | No | `http`: an HTTPS URL. `tcp`: a raw TCP service behind TLS at `address`, routed by SNI, always public (docs/public-ports.md, "Raw TCP ports"). |
| `token` | `string \| null` | No | A private port's token (`ibp_...`), only in the publish response that created or rotated it. |
| `tokenUrl` | `string \| null` | No | `url` with `?_token=<token>`: the link to share. Present with `token`. |
| `url` | `string \| null` | No | `https://<port>-<sandbox>.<domain>` for `http`, `tls://<address>` for `tcp`. Empty when public ports (or raw TCP ports) are not configured. |

### processes

`machines.processes`

The sandbox's processes, live or as last seen

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.instabox.api.machines.processes({ id: 'm_k3m7q2abx5wr4hj6zpcnfa2etd' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Sandbox ID (`m_` + 26 lowercase base32 characters) or the sandbox's name (unique among your sandboxes; names never contain `_`, so the two never collide). Older servers take only the ID and answer `404` for a name. |
| `all` | `boolean` | No | Also list kernel threads and the listing's own processes. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `capturedAt` | `string \| null` | No | When the list was read. |
| `live` | `boolean \| null` | No | The list was read from the running sandbox for this request; false: the list the server stored last, for a stopped or paused sandbox. |
| `machineId` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `processes` | `object[] \| null` | No | Without `all`, kernel threads and the listing's own processes are left out. |
| `state` | `string \| null` | No | The sandbox's state now. |
| `trigger` | `string \| null` | No | What read it: a client's request, or the server just before a stop or a pause. |
| `truncated` | `boolean \| null` | No | The listing was cut; processes are missing. |

}[] | null
